--- TITLE: From all that dwell below the skies
[VERSE 1]
From all that dwell below the skies
Let the Creator's praise arise;
Let the Redeemer's name be sung,
Through every land, by every tongue.
[VERSE 2]
Eternal are thy mercies, Lord;
Eternal truth attends thy word;
Thy praise shall sound from shore to shore,
Till suns shall rise and set no more.
[VERSE 3]
Your lofty themes, ye mortals, bring,
In songs of praise divinely sing:
The great salvation loud proclaim,
And shout for joy the Savior's name.
[VERSE 4]
In every land begin the song;
To every land the strains belong;
In cheerful sounds all voices raise,
And till the world with loudest praise.

--- TITLE: I sing th' almighty power of God
[VERSE 1]
I sing th' almighty power of God,
That made the mountains rise,
That spread the flowing seas abroad,
And built the lofty skies.
[VERSE 2]
I sing the wisdom that ordained
The sun to rule the day;
The moon shines full at his command,
And all the stars obey.
[VERSE 3]
I sing the goodness of the Lord,
That filled the earth with food;
He formed the creatures with his word,
And then pronounced them good.
[VERSE 4]
Lord! how thy wonders are displayed
Where'er I turn mine eye!
If I survey the ground I tread,
Or gaze upon the sky.

--- TITLE: Ere mountains reared their forms sublime
[VERSE 1]
Ere mountains reared their forms sublime,
Or heaven and earth in order stood,
Before the birth of ancient time,
From everlasting thou art God.
[VERSE 2]
A thousand ages in their flight
With thee are as a fleeting day;
Past, present, future, to thy sight
At once their various scenes display.
[VERSE 3]
But our brief life's a shadowy dream--
A passing thought, that soon is o'er;
That fades with morning's earliest beam,
And fills the musing mind no more.
[VERSE 4]
To us, O Lord, the wisdom give,
Each passing moment so to spend,
That we at length with thee may live
Where life and bliss shall never end.

--- TITLE: Eternal Wisdom! thee we praise
[VERSE 1]
Eternal Wisdom! thee we praise,
Thee the creation sings;
With thy loved name, rocks, hills, and seas,
And heaven's high palace rings.
[VERSE 2]
Thy hand, how wide it spread the sky!
How glorious to behold!
Tinged with a blue of heavenly dye,
And starred with sparkling gold.
[VERSE 3]
Infinite strength and equal skill
Shine through the worlds abroad;
Our souls with vast amazement fill,
And speak the builder--God.
[VERSE 4]
But the sweet beauties of thy grace
Our softer passions move;
Pity divine, in Jesus' face,
We see, adore, and love.

--- TITLE: Ah! how shall fallen man
[VERSE 1]
Ah! how shall fallen man
Be just before his God?
If he contend in righteousness,
We fall beneath his rod.
[VERSE 2]
If he our ways should mark
With strict inquiring eyes,
Could we for one of thousand faults
A just excuse devise?
[VERSE 3]
All-seeing, powerful God,
Who can with thee contend?
Or who that tries the unequal strife,
Shall prosper in the end?
[VERSE 4]
Ah! how shall guilty man
Contend with such a God?
None, none can meet him and escape,
But through the Savior's blood.

--- TITLE: How heavy is the night
[VERSE 1]
How heavy is the night
That hangs upon our eyes,
Till Christ with his reviving light
Over our souls arise.
[VERSE 2]
Our guilty spirits dread
To meet the wrath of heaven;
But, in his righteousness arrayed,
We see our sins forgiven.
[VERSE 3]
The powers of hell agree
To hold our souls in vain;
He sets the sons of bondage free,
And breaks the cursed chain.
[VERSE 4]
Lord, we adore thy ways
To bring us near to God,
Thy sovereign power, thy healing grace,
And thine atoning blood.

--- TITLE: Salvation! oh, the joyful sound
[VERSE 1]
Salvation! oh, the joyful sound!
What pleasure to our ears;
A sovereign balm for every wound,
A cordial for our fears.
[VERSE 2]
Salvation! let the echo fly
The spacious earth around,
While all the armies of the sky
Conspire to raise the sound.
[VERSE 3]
Salvation! O thou bleeding Lamb!
To Thee the praise belongs:
Salvation shall inspire our hearts,
And dwell upon our tongues.

--- TITLE: The Lord my Shepherd is
[VERSE 1]
The Lord my Shepherd is;
I shall be well supplied:
Since he is mine, and I am his,
What can I want beside?
[VERSE 2]
He leads me to the place
Where heavenly pasture grows,
Where living waters gently pass,
And full salvation flows.
[VERSE 3]
If e'er I go astray,
He doth my soul reclaim,
And guides me, in his own right way,
For his most holy name.
[VERSE 4]
While he affords his aid,
I cannot yield to fear;
Tho' I should walk thro' death's dark shade,
My Shepherd's with me there.

--- TITLE: With stately towers and bulwarks strong
[VERSE 1]
With stately towers and bulwarks strong,
Unrivaled and alone,
Loved theme of many a sacred song;
God's holy city shone.
[VERSE 2]
Thus fair was Zion's chosen seat,
The glory of all lands;
Yet fairer and in strength complete,
The Christian temple stands.
[VERSE 3]
The faithful of each clime and age
This glorious church compose;
Built on a Rock, with idle rage
The threat'ning tempest blows.
[VERSE 4]
Fear not; though hostile bands alarm,
Thy God is thy defense;
And weak and powerless every arm
Against Omnipotence.

--- TITLE: 'Twas on that dark, that dreadful night
[VERSE 1]
'Twas on that dark, that dreadful night,
When powers of earth and hell arose
Against the Son of God's delight,
And friends betrayed him to his foes.
[VERSE 2]
Before the mournful scene began
He took the bread, and blessed and brake;
What love thro' all his actions ran!
What wondrous words of grace he spake!
[VERSE 3]
"This is my body, broke for sin;
Receive and eat the living food;"
Then took the cup, and blessed the wine:
"'Tis the new cov'nant in my blood."
[VERSE 4]
"Do this," he cried, "till time shall end,
In mem'ry of your dying Friend;
Meet at the table, and record
The love of your departed Lord."
[VERSE 5]
Jesus, thy feast we celebrate;
We show thy death, we sing thy name
Till thou return, and we shall eat
The marriage supper of the Lamb.

--- TITLE: Unveil thy bosom, faithful tomb
[VERSE 1]
Unveil thy bosom, faithful tomb;
Take this new treasure to thy trust
And give these sacred relics room,
To slumber in the silent dust.
[VERSE 2]
Nor pain, nor grief, nor anxious fear,
Invades thy bounds; no mortal woes
Can reach the peaceful sleeper here,
While angels watch the soft repose.
[VERSE 3]
So Jesus slept; God's dying Son
Passed through the grave, and blest the bed;
Rest here, blest saint, till from his throne
The morning break, and pierce the shade.
[VERSE 4]
Break from his throne, illustrious morn;
Attend, O earth, his sovereign word;
Restore thy trust; a glorious form
Shall then arise to meet the Lord.

--- TITLE: A new song to the Lamb that was slain
[VERSE 1]
A new song to the Lamb that was slain.
Rev. 5. 6 8 9 10 12.
[VERSE 2]
Behold the glories of the Lamb
Amidst his Father's throne
Prepare new honours for his name,
And songs before unknown.
[VERSE 3]
Let elders worship at his feet,
The church adore around,
With vials full of odours sweet,
And harps of sweeter sound.
[VERSE 4]
Those are the prayers of the saints,
And these the hymns they raise:
Jesus is kind to our complaints,
He loves to hear our praise.
[VERSE 5]
Eternal Father, who shall look
Into thy secret will?
Who but the Son shall take that book
And open every seal?
[VERSE 6]
He shall fulfil thy great decrees,
The Son deserves it well;
Lo, in his hand the sovereign keys
Of heaven, and death, and hell!
[VERSE 7]
Now to the Lamb that once was slain
Be endless blessings paid;
Salvation, glory, joy remain
For ever on thy head.
[VERSE 8]
Thou hast redeem'd our souls with blood,
Hast set the pris'ners free,
Hast made us kings and priests to God,
And we shall reign with thee.
[VERSE 9]
The worlds of nature and of grace
Are put beneath thy power;
Then shorten these delaying days,
And bring the promis'd hour.

--- TITLE: The deity and humanity of Christ, John 1. 1-3 14
[VERSE 1]
The deity and humanity of Christ, John 1. 1-3 14.
Col. 9. 16. Eph. 3, 9 10.
[VERSE 2]
Ere the blue heavens were stretch'd abroad,
From everlasting was the Word;
With God he was; the Word was God,
And must divinely be ador'd.
[VERSE 3]
By his own power were all things made;
By him supported all things stand;
He is the whole creation's head,
And angels fly at his command.
[VERSE 4]
Ere sin was born, or Satan fell,
He led the host of morning stars;
(Thy generation who can tell,
Or count the number of thy years?)
[VERSE 5]
But lo, he leaves those heavenly forms,
The Word descends and dwells in clay,
That he may hold converse with worms,
Dress'd in such feeble flesh as they.
[VERSE 6]
Mortals with joy beheld his face,
Th' eternal Father's only Son;
How full of truth! how full of grace!
When thro' his eyes the Godhead shone!
[VERSE 7]
Archangels leave their high abode
To learn new mysteries here, and tell
The loves of our descending God,
The glories of Imannuel.

--- TITLE: The nativity of Christ, Luke 1. 30 &c. Luke 2, 10 &c
[VERSE 1]
The nativity of Christ, Luke 1. 30 &c. Luke 2, 10 &c.
[VERSE 2]
Behold, the grace appears,
The promise is fulfill'd;
Mary the wondrous virgin bears,
And Jesus is the child.
[VERSE 3]
The Lord, the highest God,
Calls him his only Son;
He bids him rule the lands abroad,
And gives, him David's throne.
[VERSE 4]
O'er Jacob shall he reign
With a peculiar sway;
The nations shall his grace obtain,
His kingdom ne'er decay.
[VERSE 5]
To bring the glorious news
A heavenly form appears;
He tells the shepherds of their joys,
And banishes their fears.
[VERSE 6]
"Go, humble swains," said he,
"To David's city fly;
"The promis'd infant born to-day,
"Doth in a manger lie.
[VERSE 7]
"With looks and heart serene,
"Go visit Christ your King;"
And straight, a flaming troop was seen;
The shepherds heard them sing:
[VERSE 8]
"Glory to God on high,
"And heavenly peace on earth,
"Good-will to men, to angels joy,
"At the Redeemer's birth!"
[VERSE 9]
In worship so divine
Let saints employ their tongues,
With the celestial host we join,
And loud repeat their songs:
[VERSE 10]
"Glory to God on high,
"And heavenly peace on earth,
"Good-will to men, to angels joy,
"At our Redeemer's Birth."

--- TITLE: The inward witness to Christianity, 1 John 5. 10
[VERSE 1]
The inward witness to Christianity, 1 John 5. 10.
[VERSE 2]
Questions and doubts be heard no more;
Let Christ and joy be all our theme;
His Spirit seals his gospel sure,
To every soul that trusts in him.
[VERSE 3]
Jesus, thy witness speaks within:
The mercy which thy words reveal
Refines the heart from sense and sin,
And stamps its own celestial seal.
[VERSE 4]
'Tis God's inimitable hand
That moulds and forms the heart anew;
Blasphemers can no more withstand,
But bow and own thy doctrine true.
[VERSE 5]
The guilty wretch that trusts thy blood,
Finds peace and pardon at thy cross;
The sinful soul averse to God,
Believes and loves his Maker's laws.
[VERSE 6]
Learning and wit may cease their strife,
When miracles with glory shine;
The voice that calls the dead to life
Must be almighty, and divine.

--- TITLE: Submission to afflictive providences, Job 1. 21
[VERSE 1]
Submission to afflictive providences, Job 1. 21.
[VERSE 2]
Naked as from the earth we came,
And crept to life at first,
We to the earth return again,
And mingle with our dust.
[VERSE 3]
The dear delights we here enjoy,
And fondly call our own,
Are but short favours borrow'd now,
To be repaid anon.
[VERSE 4]
'Tis God that lifts our comforts high,
Or sinks them in the grave;
He gives, and (blessed be his name!)
He takes but what he gave.
[VERSE 5]
Peace, all our angry passions, then,
Let each rebellious sigh
Be silent at his sov'reign will,
And every murmur die.
[VERSE 6]
If smiling mercy crown our lives,
Its praises shall be spread;
And we'll adore the justice too
That strikes our comforts dead.

--- TITLE: Triumph over death, Job 19. 25-27
[VERSE 1]
Triumph over death, Job 19. 25-27.
[VERSE 2]
Great God, I own thy sentence just
And nature must decay;
I yield my body to the dust
To dwell with fellow-clay.
[VERSE 3]
Yet faith may triumph o'er the grave,
And trample on the tombs:
My Jesus, my Redeemer lives,
My God, my Saviour comes.
[VERSE 4]
The mighty Conqueror shall appear
High on a royal seat,
And Death, the last of all his foes,
Lie vanquish'd at his feet.
[VERSE 5]
Tho' greedy worms devour my skin,
And gnaw my wasting flesh,
When God shall build my bones again,
He clothes them all afresh.
[VERSE 6]
Then shall I see thy lovely face
With strong immortal eyes,
And feast upon thy unknown grace
With pleasure and surprise.

--- TITLE: The invitation of the gospel; or, Spiritual
[VERSE 1]
The invitation of the gospel; or, Spiritual
food and clothing, Isa. 55. 1 &c.
[VERSE 2]
Let every mortal ear attend,
And every heart rejoice,
The trumpet of the gospel sounds
With an inviting voice.
[VERSE 3]
Ho, all ye hungry starving souls,
That feed upon the wind,
And vainly strive with earthly toys
To fill an empty mind,
[VERSE 4]
Eternal wisdom has prepar'd
A soul reviving feast,
And bids your longing appetites
The rich provision taste.
[VERSE 5]
Ho, ye that pant for living streams,
And pine away and die,
Here you may quench your raging thirst
With springs that never dry.
[VERSE 6]
Rivers of love and mercy here
In a rich ocean join;
Salvation in abundance flows,
Like floods of milk and wine.
[VERSE 7]
Ye perishing and naked poor,
Who work with mighty pain
To weave a garment of your own
That will not hide your sin,
[VERSE 8]
Come naked, and adorn your souls
In robes prepar'd by God,
Wrought by the labours of his Son,
And dy'd in his own blood.
[VERSE 9]
Dear God, the treasures of thy love
Are everlasting mines,
Deep as our helpless miseries are,
And boundless as our sins.
[VERSE 10]
The happy gates of gospel grace
Stand open night and day,
Lord, we are come to seek supplies,
And drive our wants away.

--- TITLE: The safety and protection of the church, Isa. 26. 1-6
[VERSE 1]
The safety and protection of the church, Isa. 26. 1-6.
[VERSE 2]
How honourable is the place
Where we adoring stand,
Zion the glory of the earth,
And beauty of the land!
[VERSE 3]
Bulwarks of mighty grace defend
The city where we dwell,
The walls of strong salvation made,
Defy th' assaults of hell.
[VERSE 4]
Lift up the everlasting gates,
The doors wide open fling,
Enter, ye nations, that obey
The statutes of our King.
[VERSE 5]
Here you shall taste unmingled joys,
And live in perfect peace,
You that have known Jehovah's name,
And ventur'd on his grace.
[VERSE 6]
Trust in the Lord, for ever trust,
And banish all your fears;
Strength in the Lord Jehovah dwells,
Eternal as his years.
[VERSE 7]
What tho' the rebels dwell on high,
His arm shall bring them low,
Low' as the caverns of the grave
Their lofty heads shall bow.
[VERSE 8]
On Babylon our feet shall tread
In that rejoicing hour,
The ruins of her walls shall spread
A pavement for the poor.

--- TITLE: The promises of the covenant of grace, Isa. 55. 1 2
[VERSE 1]
The promises of the covenant of grace, Isa. 55. 1 2.
Zech. 13. 1. Mich. 7. 19. Ezek. 36. 25 &c.
[VERSE 2]
In vain we lavish out our lives
To gather empty wind,
The choicest blessings earth can yield
Will starve a hungry mind.
[VERSE 3]
Come and the Lord shall feed our souls
With more substantial meat,
With such as saints in glory love,
With such as angels eat.
[VERSE 4]
Our God will every want supply,
And fill our hearts with peace;
He gives by covenant and by oath
The riches of his grace.
[VERSE 5]
Come, and he'll cleanse our spotted souls,
And wash away our stains,
In the dear fountain that his Son
Pour'd from his dying veins.
[VERSE 6]
Our guilt shall vanish all away
Tho' black as hell before;
Our sins shall sink beneath the sea,
And shall be found no more.
[VERSE 7]
And lest pollution should o'erspread
Our inward powers again,
His Spirit shall bedew our souls
Like purifying rain.
[VERSE 8]
Our heart, that flinty stubborn thing,
That terrors cannot move,
That fears no threat'nings of his wrath,
Shall be dissolv'd by love;
[VERSE 9]
Or he can take the flint away
That would not be refin'd,
And from the treasures of his grace
Bestow a softer mind.
[VERSE 10]
There shall his sacred Spirit dwell,
And deep engrave his law,
And every motion of our souls
To swift obedience draw.
[VERSE 11]
Thus will he pour salvation down,
And we shall render praise,
We the dear people of his love,
And he our God of grace.

--- TITLE: The blessedness of gospel times; or, The revelation of
[VERSE 1]
The blessedness of gospel times; or, The revelation of
Christ to Jews and Gentiles, Isa. 5. 2 7-10. Matt.
13. 16 17.
[VERSE 2]
How beauteous are their feet
Who stand on Zion's hill!
Who bring salvation on their tongues,
And words of peace reveal!
[VERSE 3]
How charming is their voice!
How sweet the tidings are!
"Zion, behold thy Saviour King,
He reigns and triumphs here."
[VERSE 4]
How happy are our ears
That hear this joyful sound,
Which kings and prophets waited for,
And sought, but never found!
[VERSE 5]
How blessed are our eyes
That see this heavenly light!
Prophets and kings desir'd it long,
But dy'd without the sight.
[VERSE 6]
The watchmen join their voice,
And tuneful notes employ;
Jerusalem breaks forth in songs,
And deserts leant the joy.
[VERSE 7]
The Lord makes bare his arm
Thro' all the earth abroad
Let every nation now behold
Their Saviour and their God.

--- TITLE: The humble enlightened, and carnal reason humbled
[VERSE 1]
The humble enlightened, and carnal reason humbled;
or, The sovereignty of grace, Luke 10. 21 22
[VERSE 2]
There was an hour when Christ rejoic'd,
And spoke his joy in words of praise;
"Father, I thank thee, mighty God,
"Lord of the earth, and heavens, and seas.
[VERSE 3]
"I thank thy sovereign power and love,
"That crowns my doctrine with success;
"And makes the babes in knowledge learn
"The heights, and breadths, and lengths of grace.
[VERSE 4]
"But all this glory lies conceal'd
"From men of prudence and of wit;
"The prince of darkness blinds their eyes,
"And their own pride resists the light.
[VERSE 5]
"Father, 'tis thus, because thy will
"Chose and ordain'd it should be so;
"T'is thy delight t' abase the proud,
"And lay the haughty scorner low.
[VERSE 6]
"There's none can know the Father right
"But those who learn it from the Son;
"Nor can the Son be well receiv'd,
But where the Father makes him known."
[VERSE 7]
Then let our souls adore our God
That deals his graces as he please,
Nor gives to mortals an account
Or of his actions, or decrees.

--- TITLE: Free grace in revealing Christ, Luke 10. 21
[VERSE 1]
Free grace in revealing Christ, Luke 10. 21.
[VERSE 2]
Jesus, the man of constant grief,
A mourner all his days;
His spirit once rejoic'd aloud,
And tun'd his joy to praise.
[VERSE 3]
"Father, I thank thy wondrous love,
"That hath reveal'd thy Son
"To men unlearned; and to babes
"Has made thy gospel known.
[VERSE 4]
"The mysteries of redeeming grace
"Are hidden from the wise,
"While pride and carnal reasonings join
"To swell and blind their eyes."
[VERSE 5]
Thus doth the Lord of heaven and earth
His great decrees fulfil,
And orders all his works of grace
By his own sovereign will.

--- TITLE: The Son of God incarnate; or, The titles and the
[VERSE 1]
The Son of God incarnate; or, The titles and the
kingdom of Christ, Isa. 9. 2 6 7.
[VERSE 2]
The lands that long in darkness lay
Now have beheld a heavenly light;
Nations that sat in death's cold shade
Are bless'd with beams divinely bright.
[VERSE 3]
The virgin's promis'd Son is born,
Behold th' expected Child appear;
What shall his names or titles be?
The Wonderful, the Counsellor.
[VERSE 4]
This infant is the mighty God
Come to be suckled and ador'd
Th' eternal Father, Prince of Peace,
The son of David, and his Lord.
[VERSE 5]
The government of earth and seas
Upon his shoulders shall be laid
His wide dominions still increase,
And honours to his name be paid.
[VERSE 6]
Jesus the holy child shall sit
High on his father David's throne,
Shall crush his foes beneath his feet,
And reign to ages yet unknown.

--- TITLE: The triumph of faith; or, Christ's unchangeable love
[VERSE 1]
The triumph of faith; or, Christ's unchangeable love,
Rom. 8. 33 &c.
[VERSE 2]
Who shall the Lord's elect condemn?
'Tis God that justifies their souls,
And mercy like a mighty stream
O'er all their sins divinely rolls.
[VERSE 3]
Who shall adjudge the saints to hell?
'Tis Christ that suffer'd in their stead,
And the salvation to fulfil,
Behold him rising from the dead.
[VERSE 4]
He lives, he lives, and sits above,
For ever interceding there:
Who shall divide us from his love?
Or what should tempt us to despair?
[VERSE 5]
Shall persecution, or distress,
Famine, or sword, or nakedness?
He that hath lov'd us bears us thro',
And makes us more than conquerors too.
[VERSE 6]
Faith hath an overcoming power,
It triumphs in the dying hour;
Christ is our life, our joy, our hope,
Nor can we sink with such a prop.
[VERSE 7]
Not all that men on earth can do,
Nor powers on high, nor powers below,
Shall cause his mercy to remove,
Or wean our hearts from Christ our love.

--- TITLE: Our own weakness, and Christ our strength
[VERSE 1]
Our own weakness, and Christ our strength,
[VERSE 2]
Cor. 12. 7 9 10.
[VERSE 3]
Let me but hear my Saviour say,
"Strength shall be equal to thy day,"
Then I rejoice in deep distress,
Leaning on all-sufficient Grace.
[VERSE 4]
I glory in infirmity,
That Christ's own power may rest on me;
When I am weak, then am I strong,
Grace is my shield, and Christ my song.
[VERSE 5]
I can do all things, or can bear
All sufferings, if my Lord be there;
Sweet pleasures mingle with the pains,
While his left hand my head sustains.
[VERSE 6]
But if the Lord be once withdrawn,
And we attempt the work alone,
When new temptations spring and rise
We find how great our weakness is.
[VERSE 7]
So Samson, when his hair was lost,
Met the Philistines to his cost;
Shook his vain limbs with sad surprise,
Made feeble fight, and lost his eyes.

--- TITLE: Hosanna to Christ, Matt. 21. 9. Luke 19. 38 40
[VERSE 1]
Hosanna to Christ, Matt. 21. 9. Luke 19. 38 40.
[VERSE 2]
Hosanna to the royal Son
Of David's ancient line,
His natures two, his person one,
Mysterious and divine.
[VERSE 3]
The root of David here we find,
And offspring is the same;
Eternity and time are join'd
In our Immanuel's name.
[VERSE 4]
Bless'd he that comes to wretched men
With peaceful news from heaven;
Hosannas of the highest strain
To Christ the Lord be given.
[VERSE 5]
Let mortals ne'er refuse to take
Th' hosanna on their tongues,
Lest rocks and stones should rise, and break
Their silence into songs.

--- TITLE: Victory over death, 1 Cor. 15, 55 &c
[VERSE 1]
Victory over death, 1 Cor. 15, 55 &c.
[VERSE 2]
O for an overcoming faith
To cheer my dying hours,
To triumph o'er the monster Death,
And all his frightful powers.
[VERSE 3]
Joyful with all the strength I have
My quivering lips should sing,
"Where is thy boasted victory, Grave?
And where the monster's sting?"
[VERSE 4]
If sin be pardon'd I'm secure,
Death hath no sting beside;
The law gives sin its damning power,
But Christ, my ransom, died.
[VERSE 5]
Now to the God of victory
Immortal thanks be paid,
Who makes us conquerors while we die,
Thro' Christ our living head.

--- TITLE: Blessed are the dead that die in the Lord, Rev. 14. 13
[VERSE 1]
Blessed are the dead that die in the Lord, Rev. 14. 13.
[VERSE 2]
Hear what the voice from heaven proclaims,
For all the pious dead,
Sweet is the savour of their names,
And soft their sleeping bed.
[VERSE 3]
They die in Jesus, and are bless'd;
How kind their slumbers are!
From sufferings and from sins releas'd,
And freed from every snare.
[VERSE 4]
Far from this world of toil and strife,
They're present with the Lord;
The labours of their mortal life
End in a large reward.

--- TITLE: The song of Simeon; or, Death made desirable
[VERSE 1]
The song of Simeon; or, Death made desirable,
Luke 2. 27 &c.
[VERSE 2]
Lord at thy temple we appear,
As happy Simeon came,
And hope to meet our Saviour here;
O make our joys the same!
[VERSE 3]
With what divine and vast delight
The good old man was fill'd,
When fondly in his wither'd arms
He clasp'd the holy child!
[VERSE 4]
"Now I can leave this world," he cry'd,
"Behold thy servant dies,
"I've seen thy great salvation, Lord,
"And close my peaceful eyes.
[VERSE 5]
"This is the light prepar'd to shine
"Upon the Gentile lands,
"Thine Israel's glory, and their hope
"To break their slavish bands."
[VERSE 6]
Jesus, the vision of thy face
Hath overpowering charms
Scarce shall I feel death's cold embrace
If Christ be in my arms.
[VERSE 7]
Then while ye hear my heart-strings break,
How sweet my minutes roll!
A mortal paleness on my cheek,
And glory in my soul.

--- TITLE: Spiritual apparel; namely, the robe of righteousness
[VERSE 1]
Spiritual apparel; namely, the robe of righteousness,
and garments of salvation, Isa. 61. 10.
[VERSE 2]
Awake, my heart, arise, my tongue,
Prepare a tuneful voice,
In God the life of all my joys,
Aloud will I rejoice.
[VERSE 3]
'Tis he adorn'd my naked soul,
And made salvation mine,
Upon a poor polluted worm
He makes his graces shine.
[VERSE 4]
And lest the shadow of a spot
Should on my soul be found,
He took the robe the Saviour wrought,
And cast it all around.
[VERSE 5]
How far the heavenly robe exceeds
What earthly princes wear!
These ornaments, how bright they shine!
How white the garments are!
[VERSE 6]
The Spirit wrought my faith and love,
And hope, and every grace;
But Jesus spent his life to work
The robe of righteousness.
[VERSE 7]
Strangely, my soul, art thou array'd
By the great sacred Three:
In sweetest harmony of praise
Let all thy powers agree.

--- TITLE: A vision of the kingdom of Christ among men
[VERSE 1]
A vision of the kingdom of Christ among men,
Rev. 21. 1-4.
[VERSE 2]
Lo, what a glorious sight appears
To our believing eyes!
The earth and sea are pass'd away,
And the old rolling skies.
[VERSE 3]
From the third heaven where God resides,
That holy happy place,
The New Jerusalem comes down
Adorn'd with shining grace.
[VERSE 4]
Attending angels shout for joy,
And the bright armies sing,
"Mortals, behold the sacred seat
"Of your descending King.
[VERSE 5]
"The God of glory down to men
"Removes his blest abode,
"Men the dear objects of his grace,
"And he the loving God.
[VERSE 6]
"His own soft hand shall wipe the tears
"From every weeping eye,
"And pains, and groans, and griefs, and fears,
"And death itself shall die."
[VERSE 7]
How long, dear Saviour, O how long,
Shall this bright hour delay!
Fly swifter round, ye wheels of time,
And bring the welcome day.

--- TITLE: Flesh and Spirit. Rom. 8. 1
[VERSE 1]
Flesh and Spirit. Rom. 8. 1.
[VERSE 2]
What vain desires, and passions vain,
Attend this mortal clay!
Oft have they pierc'd my soul with pain,
And drawn my heart astray.
[VERSE 3]
How have I wander'd from my God,
And following sin and shame
In this vile world of flesh and blood
Defil'd my nobler frame!
[VERSE 4]
For ever blessed be thy grace
That form'd my soul anew,
And made it of an heaven-born race,
Thy glory to pursue.
[VERSE 5]
My spirit holds perpetual war,
And wrestles and complains;
But views the happy moment near
That shall dissolve its chains.
[VERSE 6]
Cheerful in death I close my eyes,
To part with every lust;
And charge my flesh whene'er it rise
To leave them in the dust.
[VERSE 7]
My purer spirit shall not fear
To put this body on:
Its tempting powers no more are there,
Its lusts and passions gone.

--- TITLE: A hopeful youth falling short of heaven, Mark 10. 21
[VERSE 1]
A hopeful youth falling short of heaven, Mark 10. 21.
[VERSE 2]
Must all the charms of nature then
So hopeless to salvation prove?
Can hell demand, can heaven condemn
The man whom Jesus deigns to love?
[VERSE 3]
The man who sought the ways of truth,
Paid friends and neighbours all their due,
(A modest, sober, lovely youth)
And thought he wanted nothing now.
[VERSE 4]
But mark the change! thus spake the Lord,
"Come, part with earth for heaven to-day,"
The youth astonish'd at the word,
In silent sadness went his way.
[VERSE 5]
Poor virtues that he boasted so,
This test unable to endure;
Let Christ, and grace, and glory go
To make his land and money sure.
[VERSE 6]
Ah foolish choice of treasures here!
Ah fatal love of tempting gold!
Must this base world be bought so dear?
Are life and heaven so cheaply sold?
[VERSE 7]
In vain the charms of nature shine,
If this vile passion govern me:
Transform my soul, O love divine,
And make me part with all for thee.

--- TITLE: The rich sinner dying, Psalm 49. 6 9. Eccl. 8. 8
[VERSE 1]
The rich sinner dying, Psalm 49. 6 9. Eccl. 8. 8.
Job 3. 14 15.
[VERSE 2]
In vain the wealthy mortals toil,
And heap their shining dust in vain,
Look down and scorn the humble poor,
And boast their lofty hills of gain.
[VERSE 3]
Their golden cordials cannot ease
Their pained hearts or aching heads,
Nor fright nor bribe approaching death
From glittering roofs and downy beds.
[VERSE 4]
The lingering, the unwilling soul
The dismal summons must obey,
And bid a long a sad farewell
To the pale lump of lifeless clay.
[VERSE 5]
Thence they are huddled to the grave,
Where kings and slaves have equal thrones;
Their bones without distinction lie
Amongst the heap of meaner bones.
The rest referred to Psalm 49.

--- TITLE: A vision of the Lamb, Rev 5. 6-9
[VERSE 1]
A vision of the Lamb, Rev 5. 6-9.
[VERSE 2]
All mortal vanities, be gone,
Nor tempt my eyes, nor tire my ears,
Behold amidst th' eternal throne
A vision of the Lamb appears.
2[Glory his fleecy robe adorns,
Mark'd with the bloody death he bore;
Seven are his eyes, and seven his horns,
To speak his wisdom and his power.
[VERSE 3]
Lo, he receives a sealed book
From him that sits upon the throne:
Jesus, my Lord, prevails to look
On dark decrees, and things unknown.
[VERSE 4]
All the assembling saints around
Fall worshipping before the Lamb,
And in new songs of gospel-sound
Address their honours to his name,
[VERSE 5]
The joy, the shout, the harmony
Flies o'er the everlasting hills,
"Worthy art thou alone," they cry,
"To read the book, to loose the seals."
[VERSE 6]
Our voices join the heavenly strain,
And with transporting pleasure sing,
"Worthy the Lamb that once was slain,
"To be our teacher and our king!"
[VERSE 7]
His words of prophecy reveal
Eternal counsels, deep designs;
His grace and vengeance shall fulfil
The peaceful and the dreadful lines.
[VERSE 8]
Thou hast redeem'd our souls from hell
'With thine invaluable blood;
And wretches that did once rebel
Are now made favourites of their Cod.
[VERSE 9]
Worthy for ever is the Lord,
That died for treasons not his own,
By every tongue to be ador'd,
And dwell upon his Father's throne.

--- TITLE: Hope of heaven by the resurrection of Christ
[VERSE 1]
Hope of heaven by the resurrection of Christ,
[VERSE 2]
Peter 1. 3-5.
[VERSE 3]
Bless'd be the everlasting God,
The Father of our Lord,
Be his abounding mercy prais'd,
His majesty ador'd.
[VERSE 4]
When from the dead he rais'd his Son,
And call'd him to the sky,
He gave our souls a lively hope
That they should never die.
[VERSE 5]
What tho' our inbred sins require
Our flesh to see the dust!
Yet as the Lord our Saviour rose,
So all his followers must.
[VERSE 6]
There's an inheritance divine
Reserv'd against that day,
'Tis uncorrupted, undefil'd,
And cannot waste away.
[VERSE 7]
Saints by the power of God are kept
Till the salvation come;
We walk by faith as strangers here
Till Christ shall call us home.

--- TITLE: Assurance of heaven; or, A saint prepared to die
[VERSE 1]
Assurance of heaven; or, A saint prepared to die,
[VERSE 2]
Tim. 4. 6 7 8 18.
[VERSE 3]
Death may dissolve my body now,
And bear my spirit home;
Why do my minutes move so slow,
Nor my salvation come?
[VERSE 4]
With heavenly weapons I have fought
The battles of the Lord,
Finish'd my course, and kept the faith,
And wait the sure reward.
[VERSE 5]
God has laid up in heaven for me
A crown which cannot fade;
The righteous Judge at that great day
Shall place it on my head.
[VERSE 6]
Nor hath the King of grace decreed
This prize for me alone;
But all that love and long to see
Th' appearance of his Son.
[VERSE 7]
Jesus the Lord shall guard me safe
From every ill design;
And to his heavenly kingdom keep
This feeble soul of mine.
[VERSE 8]
God is my everlasting aid
And hell shall rage in vain;
To him be highest glory paid,
And endless praise.--Amen.

--- TITLE: The triumph of Christ over the enemies of his church
[VERSE 1]
The triumph of Christ over the enemies of his church,
Isa. 63. 1 2 3 &c.
[VERSE 2]
What mighty man, or mighty God,
Comes travelling in state,
Along the Idumean road,
Away from Bozrah's gate?
[VERSE 3]
The glory of his robes proclaim
'Tis some victorious king:
"'Tis I, the just, th' Almighty One,
"That your salvation bring."
[VERSE 4]
"Why, mighty Lord," thy saints enquire,
"Why thine apparel red?
"And all thy vesture stain'd like those
"Who in the wine-press tread?"
[VERSE 5]
"I by myself have trod the press,
"And crush'd my foes alone,
"My wrath has struck the rebels dead,
"My fury stamp'd them down.
[VERSE 6]
"'Tis Edom's blood that dyes my robes
"With joyful scarlet stains,
"The triumph that my raiment wears
"Sprung from their bleeding veins.
[VERSE 7]
"Thus shall the nations be destroy'd
"That dare insult my saints,
"I have an arm t' avenge their wrongs,
"An ear for their complaints."

--- TITLE: The ruin of Antichrist, Isa. 63. 4-7
[VERSE 1]
The ruin of Antichrist, Isa. 63. 4-7.
[VERSE 2]
"I lift my banners," saith the Lord,
"Where Antichrist has stood,
"The city of my gospel-foes
"Shall be a field of blood.
[VERSE 3]
"My heart has study'd just revenge,
"And now the day appears,
"The day of my redeem'd is come
"To wipe away their tears.
[VERSE 4]
"Quite weary is my patience grown,
"And bids my fury go
"Swift as the lightning it shall move,
"And be as fatal too.
[VERSE 5]
"I call for helpers but in vain:
"Then has my gospel none?
"Well, mine own arm has might enough
"To crush my foes alone.
[VERSE 6]
"Slaughter and my devouring sword
"Shall walk the streets around,
"Babel shall reel beneath my stroke,
"And stagger to the ground."
[VERSE 7]
Thy honours, O victorious King!
Thine own right-hand shall raise,
While we thy awful vengeance sing,
And our deliverer praise.

--- TITLE: Prayer for deliverance answered, Isa. 26. 8-12 20 21
[VERSE 1]
Prayer for deliverance answered, Isa. 26. 8-12 20 21.
[VERSE 2]
In thine own ways, O God of love,
We wait the visits of thy grace,
Our souls desire is to thy Name,
And the remembrance of thy face.
[VERSE 3]
My thoughts are searching, Lord, for thee,
'Mongst the black shades of lonesome night;
My earnest cries salute the skies
Before the dawn restore the light.
[VERSE 4]
Look, how rebellious men deride
The tender patience of my God;
But they shall see thy lifted hand,
And feel the scourges of thy rod.
[VERSE 5]
Hark, the Eternal rends the sky,
A mighty voice before him goes,
A voice of music to his friends,
But threatening thunder to his foes.
[VERSE 6]
Come, children, to your Father's arms,
Hide in the chambers of my grace,
Till the fierce storms be overblown,
And my revenging fury cease.
[VERSE 7]
My sword shall boast its thousands slain,
And drink the blood of haughty kings,
While heavenly peace around my flock
Stretches its soft and shady wings.

--- TITLE: The hidden life of a Christian, Col. 3. 3
[VERSE 1]
The hidden life of a Christian, Col. 3. 3.
[VERSE 2]
O happy soul that lives on high;
While men lie grovelling here!
His hopes are fix'd above the sky,
And faith forbids his fear.
[VERSE 3]
His conscience knows no secret stings,
While peace and joy combine
To form a life whose holy springs
Are hidden and divine.
[VERSE 4]
He waits in secret on his God;
His God in secret sees:
Let earth be all in arms abroad,
He dwells in heavenly peace.
[VERSE 5]
His pleasures rise from things unseen,
Beyond this world and time,
Where neither eyes nor ears have been,
Nor thoughts of sinners climb.
[VERSE 6]
He wants no pomp nor royal throne
To raise his figure here;
Content and pleas'd to live unknown,
Till Christ his life appear.
[VERSE 7]
He looks to heaven's eternal hill
To meet that glorious day:
But patient waits his Saviour's will
To fetch his soul away.

--- TITLE: Strength from heaven, Isa. 40. 27-30
[VERSE 1]
Strength from heaven, Isa. 40. 27-30.
[VERSE 2]
Whence do our mournful thoughts arise?
And where's our courage fled?
Has restless sin and raging hell
Struck all our comforts dead?
[VERSE 3]
Have we forgot th' almighty Name
That form'd the earth and sea?
And can an all-creating arm
Grow weary or decay?
[VERSE 4]
Treasures of everlasting might
In our Jehovah dwell;
He gives the conquest to the weak,
And treads their foes to hell.
[VERSE 5]
Mere mortal power shall fade and die,
And youthful vigour cease;
But we that wait upon the Lord
Shall feel our strength increase.
[VERSE 6]
The saints shall mount on eagles' wings
And taste the promis'd bliss,
Till their unwearied feet arrive
Where perfect pleasure is.

--- TITLE: The gospel the power of God to salvation, Rom. 1. 16
[VERSE 1]
The gospel the power of God to salvation, Rom. 1. 16.
[VERSE 2]
Cor. 1. 18 24.
[VERSE 3]
What shall the dying sinner do
That seeks relief for all his woe?
Where shall the guilty conscience find
Ease for the torment of the mind?
[VERSE 4]
How shall we get our crimes forgiven,
Or form our natures fit for heaven?
Can souls all o'er defil'd with sin
Make their own powers and passions clean?
[VERSE 5]
In vain we search, in vain we try,
Till Jesus bring his gospel nigh;
'Tis there such power and glory dwell
As saves rebellious souls from hell.
[VERSE 6]
This is the pillar of our hope
That bears our fainting spirits up;
We read the grace, we trust the word,
And find salvation in the Lord.
[VERSE 7]
Let men or angels dig the mines
Where nature's golden treasure shines,
Brought near the doctrine of the cross
All nature's gold appears but dross.
[VERSE 8]
Should vile blasphemers with disdain
Pronounce the truths of Jesus vain,
I'll meet the scandal and the shame,
And sing and triumph in his Name.

--- TITLE: None excluded from hope, Rom. 1. 16. 1 Cor. 1. 24
[VERSE 1]
None excluded from hope, Rom. 1. 16. 1 Cor. 1. 24.
[VERSE 2]
Jesus, thy blessings are not few,
Nor is thy gospel weak;
Thy grace can melt the stubborn Jew,
And bow th' aspiring Greek.
[VERSE 3]
Wide as the reach of Satan's rage
Doth thy salvation flow;
'Tis not confin'd to sex or age,
The lofty or the low.
[VERSE 4]
While grace is offer'd to the prince,
The poor may take their share;
No mortal has a just pretence
To perish in despair.
[VERSE 5]
Be wise, ye men of strength and wit,
Nor boast your native powers;
But to his sovereign grace submit,
And glory shall be yours.
[VERSE 6]
Come, all ye vilest sinners come,
He'll form your souls anew:
His gospel and his heart have room
For rebels such as you.
[VERSE 7]
His doctrine is almighty love;
There's virtue in his Name
To turn the raven to a dove,
The lion to a lamb.

--- TITLE: Truth, sincerity, &c. Phil. 4. 8
[VERSE 1]
Truth, sincerity, &c. Phil. 4. 8.
[VERSE 2]
Let those who bear the Christian name
Their holy vows fulfil:
The saints, the followers of the Lamb,
Are men of honour still.
[VERSE 3]
True to the solemn oath they take,
Tho' to their hurt they swear;
Constant and just to all they speak,
For God and angels hear.
[VERSE 4]
Still with their lips their hearts agree,
Nor flattering words devise,
They know the God of truth can see
Thro' every false disguise.
[VERSE 5]
They hate th' appearance of a lie
In all the shapes it wears;
They live the truth; and, when they die,
Eternal life is theirs.
[VERSE 6]
While hypocrites and liars fly
Before the Judge's frown,
His faithful friends, who fear a lie,
Receive th' immortal crown.

--- TITLE: A lovely carriage
[VERSE 1]
A lovely carriage.
[VERSE 2]
O 'tis a lovely thing to see
A man of prudent heart,
Whose thoughts, and lips, and life agree
To act a useful part.
[VERSE 3]
When envy, strife, and wars begin,
In little angry souls,
Mark how the sons of peace come in,
And quench the kindling coals.
[VERSE 4]
Their minds are humble, mild, and meek,
Nor let their fury rise;
Nor passion moves their lips to speak,
Nor pride exalts their eyes.
[VERSE 5]
Their frame is prudence mix'd with love,
Good works fulfil their day:
They join the serpent with the dove,
But cast the sting away.
[VERSE 6]
Such was the Saviour of mankind;
Such pleasures he pursu'd;
His flesh and blood were all refin'd,
His soul divinely good.
[VERSE 7]
Lord, can these plants of virtue grow
In such a heart as mine?
Thy grace my nature can renew,
And make my soul like thine.

--- TITLE: Zeal and Fortitude
[VERSE 1]
Zeal and Fortitude.
[VERSE 2]
Do I believe what Jesus saith,
And think the gospel true?
Lord, make me bold to own my faith,
And practise virtue too.
[VERSE 3]
Suppress my shame, subdue my fear,
Arm me with heavenly zeal,
That I may make thy power appear,
And works of praise fulfil.
[VERSE 4]
If men shall see my virtue shine,
And spread my name abroad,
Thine is the power, the praise is thine,
My Saviour and my God.
[VERSE 5]
Thus when the saints in glory meet,
Their lips proclaim thy grace;
They cast their honours at thy feet,
And own their borrow'd rays.
PAUSE.
[VERSE 6]
Are we the soldiers of the cross?
The followers of the Lamb?
And shall we fear to own his cause,
Or blush to speak his name?
[VERSE 7]
Now we must fight, if we would reign;
Increase our courage, Lord!
We'll bear the toil, endure the pain,
Supported by thy word.
[VERSE 8]
Thy saints in all this glorious war
Shall Conquer tho' they're slain;
They see the triumph from afar,
And shall with Jesus reign.
[VERSE 9]
When that illustrious day shall rise,
And all thy armies shine
In robes of victory thro' the skies,
The glory shall be thine.

--- TITLE: The universal law of equity. Matt. 8. 12
[VERSE 1]
The universal law of equity. Matt. 8. 12.
[VERSE 2]
Blessed Redeemer how divine,
How righteous is this rule of thine,
"To do to all men just the same
"As we expect or wish from them."
[VERSE 3]
This golden lesson, short and plain,
Gives not the mind nor memory pain;
And every conscience must approve
This universal law of love.
[VERSE 4]
How blest would every nation be,
Thus rul'd by love and equity!
All would be friends without a foe,
And form a paradise below.
[VERSE 5]
Jesus, forgive us, that we keep
Thy sacred law of love asleep,
No more let envy, wrath, and pride,
But thy blest maxims be our guide.

--- TITLE: God's tender care of his church, Isa. 13 &c
[VERSE 1]
God's tender care of his church, Isa. 13 &c.
[VERSE 2]
How shall my inward joys arise
And burst into a song,
Almighty love inspires my heart,
And pleasure tunes my tongue.
[VERSE 3]
God on his thirsty Sion-hill
Some mercy-drops has thrown,
And solemn oaths have bound his love
To shower salvation down.
[VERSE 4]
Why do we then indulge our fears,
Suspicions and complaints?
Is he a God, and shall his grace
Grow weary of his saints?
[VERSE 5]
Can a kind woman e'er forget
The infant of her womb,
And 'mongst a thousand tender thoughts
Her suckling have no room?
[VERSE 6]
"Yet (saith the Lord) should nature change,
"And mothers monsters prove,
"Sion still dwells upon the heart
"Of everlasting love.
[VERSE 7]
"Deep on the palms of both my hands
"I have engrav'd her name,
"My hands shall raise her ruin'd walls,
"And build her broken frame."

--- TITLE: The business and blessedness of glorified saints
[VERSE 1]
The business and blessedness of glorified saints,
Rev. 7. 13 &c.
[VERSE 2]
"What happy men, or angels these
"That all their robes are spotless white?
"Whence did this glorious troop arrive
"At the pure realms of heavenly light?"
[VERSE 3]
From tort'ring racks and burning fires,
And seas of their own blood they came;
But nobler blood has wash'd their robes,
Flowing from Christ the dying Lamb.
[VERSE 4]
Now they approach th' almighty throne,
With loud hosannas night and day,
Sweet anthems to the great Three One
Measure their blest eternity.
[VERSE 5]
No more shall hunger pain their souls,
He bids their parching thirst be gone,
And spreads the shadow of his wings
To screen them from the scorching sun.
[VERSE 6]
The Lamb that fills the middle throne
Shall shed around his milder beams,
There shall they feast on his rich love,
And drink full joys from living streams.
[VERSE 7]
Thus shall their mighty bliss renew
Thro' the vast round of endless years,
And the soft hand of sovereign grace
Heals all their wounds, and wipes their tears.

--- TITLE: The same; or, The martyrs glorified, Rev. 7. 13 &c
[VERSE 1]
The same; or, The martyrs glorified, Rev. 7. 13 &c.
[VERSE 2]
"These glorious minds, how bright they shine
"Whence all their white array?
"How came they to the happy seats
"Of everlasting day?"
[VERSE 3]
From tort'ring pains to endless joys
On fiery wheels they rode,
And strangely wash'd their raiment white
In Jesus' dying blood.
[VERSE 4]
Now they approach a spotless God,
And bow before his throne
Their warbling harps and sacred songs
Adore the Holy One.
[VERSE 5]
The unveil'd glories of his face
Amongst his saints reside,
While the rich treasure of his grace
Sees all their wants supply'd.
[VERSE 6]
Tormenting thirst shall leave their souls,
And hunger flee as fast;
The fruit of life's immortal tree
Shall be their sweet repast.
[VERSE 7]
The Lamb shall lead his heavenly flock
Where living fountains rise,
And love divine shall wipe away
The sorrows of their eyes.

--- TITLE: Divine wrath and mercy, Nahum 1, 2 &c
[VERSE 1]
Divine wrath and mercy, Nahum 1, 2 &c.
[VERSE 2]
Adore and tremble, for our God
Is a _consuming fire_;*
His jealous eyes his wrath inflame,
And raise his vengeance higher.
[VERSE 3]
Almighty vengeance how it burns!
How bright his fury glows!
Vast magazines of plagues and storms
Lie treasur'd for his foes.
[VERSE 4]
Those heaps of wrath by slow degrees
Are forced into a flame,
But kindled, O how fierce they blaze!
And rend all nature's frame.
[VERSE 5]
At his approach the mountains flee,
And seek a watery grave;
The frighted sea makes haste away,
And shrinks up every wave.
[VERSE 6]
Thro' the wide air the weighty rocks
Are swift as hailstones hurl'd:
Who dares engage his fiery rage
That shakes the solid world?
[VERSE 7]
Yet, mighty God, thy sovereign grace
Sits regent on the throne,
The refuge of thy chosen race
When wrath comes rushing down.
[VERSE 8]
Thy hand shall on rebellious kings
A fiery tempest pour,
While we beneath thy sheltering wings
Thy just revenge adore.
*Hebrews 12:29.

--- TITLE: The Christian treasure, 1 Cor. 3. 21
[VERSE 1]
The Christian treasure, 1 Cor. 3. 21.
[VERSE 2]
How vast the treasure we possess!
How rich thy bounty, King of Grace!
This world is ours, and worlds to come
Earth is our lodge, and heaven our home.
[VERSE 3]
All things are ours, the gifts of God;
The purchase of a Saviour's blood:
While the good Spirit shews us how
To use and to improve them too.
[VERSE 4]
If peace and plenty crown my days,
They help, me, Lord, to speak thy praise!
If bread of sorrows be my food,
Those sorrows work my lasting good.
[VERSE 5]
I would not change my blest estate
For all the world calls good or great:
And while my faith can keep her hold,
I envy not the sinner's gold.
[VERSE 6]
Father, I wait thy daily will;
Thou shalt divide my portion still:
Grant me on earth what seems thee best,
Till death and heaven reveal the rest.

--- TITLE: The true improvement of life
[VERSE 1]
The true improvement of life.
[VERSE 2]
And is this life prolong'd to me?
Are days and seasons given?
O let me then prepare to be
A fitter heir of heaven.
[VERSE 3]
In vain these moments shall not pass,
These golden hours be gone:
Lord, I accept thine offered grace,
I bow before thy throne.
[VERSE 4]
Now cleanse my soul from every sin,
By my Redeemer's blood:
Now let my flesh and soul begin
The honours of my God.
[VERSE 5]
Let me no more my soul beguile
With sin's deceitful toys:
Let cheerful hope increasing still
Approach to heavenly joys.
[VERSE 6]
My thankful lips shall loud proclaim
The wonders of thy praise,
And spread the savour of thy Name
Where'er I spend my days.
[VERSE 7]
On earth let my example shine,
And when I leave this state,
May heaven receive this soul of mine
To bliss supremely great.

--- TITLE: The last judgment, Rev. 21. 5-8
[VERSE 1]
The last judgment, Rev. 21. 5-8.
[VERSE 2]
See where the great incarnate God
Fills a majestic throne,
While from the skies his awful voice
Bears the last judgment down.
[VERSE 3]
"I am the first, and I the last,
"Thro' endless years the same;
"I AM is my memorial still,
"And my eternal name.
[VERSE 4]
"Such favours as a God can give
"My royal grace bestows;
"Ye thirsty souls come taste the streams
"Where life and pleasure flows.
[VERSE 5]
"The saint that triumphs o'er his sins,
"I'll own him for a son,
"The whole creation shall reward
"The conquests he has won.
[VERSE 6]
"But bloody hands and hearts unclean,
"And all the lying race,
"The faithless and the scoffing crew,
"That spurn at offer'd grace,
[VERSE 7]
"They shall be taken from my sight,
"Bound fast in iron chains,
"And headlong plung'd into the lake
"Where fire and darkness reigns."
[VERSE 8]
O may I stand before the Lamb,
When earth and seas are fled!
And hear the Judge pronounce my name
With blessings on my head!
[VERSE 9]
May I with those for ever dwell
Who here were my delight,
While sinners banish'd down to hell,
No more offend my sight.

--- TITLE: The privileges of the living above the dead
[VERSE 1]
The privileges of the living above the dead.
[VERSE 2]
Awake, my zeal, awake, my love,
To serve my Saviour here below,
In works which perfect saints above
And holy angels cannot do.
[VERSE 3]
Awake my charity, to feed
The hungry soul, and clothe the poor:
In heaven are found no sons of need,
There all these duties are no more.
[VERSE 4]
Subdue thy passions, O my soul!
Maintain the fight, thy work pursue,
Daily thy rising sins control,
And be thy victories ever new.
[VERSE 5]
The land of triumph lies on high,
There are no foes t' encounter there:
Lord, I would conquer till I die,
And finish all the glorious war.
[VERSE 6]
Let every flying hour confess
I gain thy gospel fresh renown;
And when my life and labour cease,
May I possess the promis'd crown.

--- TITLE: Death of kindred improved
[VERSE 1]
Death of kindred improved.
[VERSE 2]
Must friends and kindred drop and die?
And helpers be withdrawn?
While sorrow with a weeping eye
Counts up our comforts gone?
[VERSE 3]
Be thou our comfort, mighty God!
Our helper and our friend:
Nor leave us in this dangerous road,
Till all our trials end.
[VERSE 4]
O may our feet pursue the way
Our pious fathers led!
With love and holy zeal obey
The counsels of the dead.
[VERSE 5]
Let us be wean'd from all below,
Let hope our grief expel,
While death invites our souls to go
Where our best kindred dwell.

--- TITLE: The Christian race, Isa. 40. 28-31
[VERSE 1]
The Christian race, Isa. 40. 28-31.
[VERSE 2]
Awake, our souls, away, our fears,
Let every trembling thought be gone;
Awake and run the heavenly race,
And put a cheerful courage on.
[VERSE 3]
True, 'tis a strait and thorny road,
And mortal spirits tire and faint;
But they forget the mighty God
That feeds the strength of every saint.
[VERSE 4]
Thee, mighty God, whose matchless power
Is ever new and ever young,
And firm endures while endless years
Their everlasting circles run.
[VERSE 5]
From thee the overflowing spring,
Our souls shall drink a fresh supply,
While such as trust their native strength
Shall melt away, and drop and die.
[VERSE 6]
Swift as an eagle cuts the air
We'll mount aloft to thine abode,
On wings of love our souls shall fly,
Nor tire amidst the heavenly road.

--- TITLE: The works of Moses and the Lamb, Rev. 15. 3
[VERSE 1]
The works of Moses and the Lamb, Rev. 15. 3.
[VERSE 2]
How strong thine arm is, mighty God,
Who would not fear thy Name!
Jesus, how sweet thy graces are!
Who would not love the Lamb!
[VERSE 3]
He has done more than Moses did,
Our Prophet and our King;
From bonds of hell he freed our souls,
And taught our lips to sing.
[VERSE 4]
In the Red-sea by Moses' hand
Th' Egyptian host was drown'd;
But his own blood hides all our sins,
And guilt no more is found.
[VERSE 5]
When thro' the desert Israel went,
With manna they were fed;
Our Lord invites us to his flesh,
And calls it living bread.
[VERSE 6]
Moses beheld the promis'd land,
Yet never reach'd the place;
But Christ shall bring his followers home
To see his Father's face.
[VERSE 7]
Then shall our love and joy be full,
And feel a warmer flame;
And sweeter voices tune the song
Of Moses and the Lamb.

--- TITLE: The song of Zacharias, and the message of John
[VERSE 1]
The song of Zacharias, and the message of John
the Baptist; or, Light and salvation by Jesus Christ,
Luke 1. 68 &c. John 1. 29. 32.
[VERSE 2]
Now be the God of Israel bless'd
Who makes his truth appear,
His mighty hand fulfils his word,
And all the oaths he sware.
[VERSE 3]
Now he bedews old David's root
With blessings from the skies;
He makes the branch of promise grow,
The promis'd horn arise.
[VERSE 4]
John was the prophet of the Lord
To go before his face,
The herald which our Saviour God
Sent to prepare his ways.
[VERSE 5]
He makes the great salvation known,
He speaks of pardon'd sins;
While grace divine and heavenly love
In its own glory shines.
[VERSE 6]
"Behold the Lamb of God," he cries,
"That takes our guilt away:
"I saw the Spirit o'er his head
"On his baptizing day.
[VERSE 7]
"Be every vale exalted high,
"Sink every mountain low,
"The proud must stoop, and humble souls
"Shall his salvation know.
[VERSE 8]
"The heathen realms with Israel's land
"Shall join in sweet accord;
"And all that's born of man shall see
"The glory of the Lord.
[VERSE 9]
"Behold the morning star arise,
"Ye that in darkness sit;
"He marks the path that leads to peace,
"And guides our doubtful feet."

--- TITLE: Persevering grace, Jude 24 25
[VERSE 1]
Persevering grace, Jude 24 25.
[VERSE 2]
To God the only wise,
Our Saviour and our King,
Let all the saints below the skies
Their humble praises bring.
[VERSE 3]
'Tis his almighty love,
His counsel, and his care,
Preserves us safe from sin and death,
And every hurtful snare.
[VERSE 4]
He will present our souls
Unblemish'd and complete,
Before the glory of his face,
With joys divinely great.
[VERSE 5]
Then all the chosen seed
Shall meet around the throne,
Shall bless the conduct of his grace,
And make his wonders known.
[VERSE 6]
To our Redeemer God
Wisdom and power belongs,
Immortal crowns of majesty,
And everlasting songs.

--- TITLE: Baptism, Matt. 28. 19. Acts 2. 38
[VERSE 1]
Baptism, Matt. 28. 19. Acts 2. 38.
[VERSE 2]
'Twas the commission of our Lord,
"Go, teach the nations, and baptize;"
The nations have receiv'd the word
Since he ascended to the skies.
[VERSE 3]
He sits upon th' eternal hills,
With grace and pardon in his hands,
And sends his covenant with the seals,
To bless the distant British lands.
[VERSE 4]
"Repent, and be baptiz'd, (he saith)
For the remission of your sins;"
And thus our sense assists our faith,
And shews us what his gospel means.
[VERSE 5]
Our souls he washes in his blood,
As water makes the body clean;
And the good Spirit from our God
Descends like purifying rain.
[VERSE 6]
Thus we engage ourselves to thee,
And seal our covenant with the Lord:
O may the great eternal Three
In heaven our solemn vows record!

--- TITLE: The Holy Scriptures, Heb. 1. 1 2. 2 Tim. 3. 15 16
[VERSE 1]
The Holy Scriptures, Heb. 1. 1 2. 2 Tim. 3. 15 16.
Psalm 147. 19 20.
[VERSE 2]
God who in various methods told
His mind and will to saints of old,
Sent down his Son, with truth and grace,
To teach us in these latter days.
[VERSE 3]
Our nation reads the written word,
That book of life, that sure record:
The bright inheritance of heaven
Is by the sweet conveyance given.
[VERSE 4]
God's kindest thoughts are here express'd,
Able to make us wise and bless'd;
The doctrines are divinely true,
Fit for reproof, and comfort too.
[VERSE 5]
Ye British isles, who read his love
In long epistles from above,
(He hath not sent his sacred word
To every land) Praise ye the Lord.

--- TITLE: Electing grace; or, Saints beloved in Christ
[VERSE 1]
Electing grace; or, Saints beloved in Christ,
Eph. 1. 3 &c.
[VERSE 2]
Jesus, we bless thy Father's Name;
Thy God and ours are both the same:
What heavenly blessings from his throne
Flow down to sinners thro' his Son!
[VERSE 3]
"Christ be my first elect," he said,
Then chose our souls in Christ our head,
Before he gave the mountains birth,
Or laid foundations for the earth.
[VERSE 4]
Thus did eternal love begin
To raise us up from death and sin;
Our characters were then decreed,
"Blameless in love, a holy seed."
[VERSE 5]
Predestinated to be sons,
Born by degrees, but chose at once;
A new regenerated race
To praise the glory of his grace.
[VERSE 6]
With Christ our Lord we share our part
In the affections of his heart,
Nor shall our souls be thence remov'd
Till he forgets his first belov'd.

--- TITLE: Hesekiah's song; or, Sickness and recovery, Isa. 38.9 &c
[VERSE 1]
Hesekiah's song; or, Sickness and recovery, Isa. 38.9 &c.
[VERSE 2]
When we are rais'd from deep distress
Our God deserves a song;
We take the pattern of our praise
From Hezekiah's tongue.
[VERSE 3]
The gates of the devouring grave
Are open'd wide in vain,
If he that holds the keys of death
Commands them fast again.
[VERSE 4]
Pains of the flesh are wont t' abuse
Our minds with slavish fears,
"Our days are past, and we shall lose
"The remnant of our years."
[VERSE 5]
We chatter with a swallow's voice,
Or like a dove we mourn,
With bitterness instead of joys,
Afflicted and forlorn.
[VERSE 6]
Jehovah speaks the healing word,
And no disease withstands;
Fevers and plagues obey the Lord,
And fly at his commands.
[VERSE 7]
If half the strings of life should break,
He can our frame restore;
He casts our sins behind his back,
And they are found no more.

--- TITLE: The song of Moses and the Lamb; or, Babylon falling
[VERSE 1]
The song of Moses and the Lamb; or, Babylon falling,
Rev. 15. 3. and chap. 16. 19. and 17. 6.
[VERSE 2]
We sing the glories of thy love,
We sound thy dreadful Name;
The Christian church unites the songs
Of Moses and the Lamb.
[VERSE 3]
Great God, how wondrous are thy works
Of vengeance and of grace!
Thou King of saints, Almighty Lord,
How just and true thy ways!
[VERSE 4]
Who dares refuse to fear thy Name,
Or worship at thy throne?
Thy judgments speak thine holiness
Thro' all the nations known.
[VERSE 5]
Great Babylon, that rules the earth,
Drunk with the martyrs' blood,
Her crimes shall speedily awake
The fury of our God.
[VERSE 6]
The cup of wrath is ready mix'd,
And she must drink the dregs;
Strong is the Lord her sovereign Judge,
And shall fulfil the plagues.

--- TITLE: Original sin; or, The first and second Adam
[VERSE 1]
Original sin; or, The first and second Adam,
Rom. 5. 12. Psalm 51. 5. Job 11. 4.
[VERSE 2]
Backward with humble shame we look
On our original;
How is our nature dash'd and broke
In our first father's fall!
[VERSE 3]
To all that's good averse and blind,
But prone to all that's ill
What dreadful darkness veils our mind!
How obstinate our will!
[VERSE 4]
Conceiv'd in sin (O wretched state!)
Before we draw our breath,
The first young pulse begins to beat
Iniquity and death.
[VERSE 5]
How strong in our degenerate blood
The old corruption reigns,
And, mingling with the crooked flood,
Wanders thro' all our veins!
[VERSE 6]
Wild and unwholesome as the root
Will all the branches be;
How can we hope for living fruit
From such a deadly tree?
[VERSE 7]
What mortal power from things unclean
Can pure productions bring?
Who can command a vital stream
From an infected spring?
[VERSE 8]
Yet, mighty God, thy wondrous love
Can make our nature clean,
While Christ and grace prevail above
The tempter, death, and sin.
[VERSE 9]
The second Adam shall restore
The ruins of the first,
Hosanna to that sovereign power
That new creates our dust.

--- TITLE: The devil vanquished; or, Michael's war with the
[VERSE 1]
The devil vanquished; or, Michael's war with the
dragon, Rev. 12. 7.
[VERSE 2]
Let mortal tongues attempt to sing
The wars of heaven, when Michael stood
Chief general of th' Eternal King,
And fought the battles of our God.
[VERSE 3]
Against the dragon and his host
The armies of the Lord prevail:
In vain they rage, in vain they boast,
Their courage sinks, their weapons fail.
[VERSE 4]
Down to the earth was Satan thrown,
Down to the earth his legions fell;
Then was the trump of triumph blown,
And shook the dreadful deeps of hell.
[VERSE 5]
Now is the hour of darkness past,
Christ hath assum'd his reigning power;
Behold the great accuser cast
Down from the skies, to rise no more.
[VERSE 6]
'Twas by thy blood immortal Lamb,
Thine armies trod the tempter down;
'Twas by thy word and powerful Name,
They gain'd the battle and renown.
[VERSE 7]
Rejoice, ye heavens; let every star
Shine with new glories round the sky;
Saints, while ye sing the heavenly war,
Raise your Deliverer's name on high.

--- TITLE: Babylon fallen, Rev. 18. 20 21
[VERSE 1]
Babylon fallen, Rev. 18. 20 21.
[VERSE 2]
In Gabriel's hand a mighty stone
Lies, a fair type of Babylon
''Prophets, rejoice, and, all ye saints,
"God shall avenge your long complaints."
[VERSE 3]
He said, and dreadful as he stood,
He sunk the milstone in the flood:
"Thus terribly shall Babel fall;
"Thus, and no more be found at all."

--- TITLE: The virgin Mary's song; or, The promised Messiah
[VERSE 1]
The virgin Mary's song; or, The promised Messiah
born, Luke 1. 46 &c.
[VERSE 2]
Our souls shall magnify the Lord,
In God the Saviour we rejoice;
While we repeat the Virgin's song,
May the same spirit tune our voice.
[VERSE 3]
The Highest saw her low estate,
And mighty things his hand hath done:
His overshadowing power and grace
Makes her the mother of his Son.
[VERSE 4]
Let every nation call her bless'd,
And endless years prolong her fame;
But God alone must be ador'd;
Holy and reverend is his Name.
[VERSE 5]
To those that fear and trust the Lord
His mercy stands for ever sure:
From age to age his promise lives,
And the performance is secure.
[VERSE 6]
He spake to Abr'am and his seed,
"In thee shall all the earth be bless'd;"
The memory of that ancient word
Lay long in his eternal breast.
[VERSE 7]
But now no more shall Israel wait,
No more the Gentiles lie forlorn:
Lo the desire of nations comes,
Behold the promis'd seed is born.

--- TITLE: Christ our high Priest and King, and Christ coming
[VERSE 1]
Christ our high Priest and King, and Christ coming
to judgment, Rev. 1. 5 6 7.
[VERSE 2]
Now to the Lord that makes us know
The wonders of his dying love,
Be humble honours paid below,
And strains of nobler praise above.
[VERSE 3]
'Twas he that cleans'd our foulest sins,
And wash'd us in his richest blood;
'Tis he that makes us priests and kings,
And brings us rebels near to God.
[VERSE 4]
To Jesus our atoning priest,
To Jesus our superior king,
Be everlasting power confess'd,
And every tongue his glory sing.
[VERSE 5]
Behold, on flying clouds he comes,
And every eye shall see him move;
Tho' with our sins we pierc'd him once,
Then he displays his pardoning love.
[VERSE 6]
The unbelieving world shall wail
While we rejoice to see the day:
Come, Lord; nor let thy promise fail,
Nor let thy chariots long delay.

--- TITLE: Christ Jesus, the Lamb of God, worshipped by all
[VERSE 1]
Christ Jesus, the Lamb of God, worshipped by all
the creation, Rev. 5. 11 12 13.
[VERSE 2]
Come let us join our cheerful songs
With angels round the throne;
Ten thousand thousand are their tongues,
But all their joys are one.
[VERSE 3]
"Worthy the Lamb that dy'd," they cry,
"To be exalted thus:"
"Worthy the Lamb," our lips reply,
"For he was slain for us."
[VERSE 4]
Jesus is worthy to receive
Honour and power divine;
And blessings more than we can give,
Be, Lord, for ever thine.
[VERSE 5]
Let all that dwell above the sky,
And air, and earth, and seas,
Conspire to lift thy glories high,
And speak thine endless praise.
[VERSE 6]
The whole creation join in one
To bless the sacred Name
Of him that sits upon the throne,
And to adore the Lamb.

--- TITLE: Christ's humiliation and exaltation, Rev. 5. 12
[VERSE 1]
Christ's humiliation and exaltation, Rev. 5. 12.
[VERSE 2]
What equal honours shall we bring
To thee, O Lord our God, the Lamb,
When all the notes that angels sing
Are far inferior to thy Name?
[VERSE 3]
Worthy is he that once was slain,
The Prince of Peace that groan'd and dy'd,
Worthy to rise, and live, and reign
At his Almighty Father's side.
[VERSE 4]
Power and dominion are his due,
Who stood condemn'd at Pilate's bar:
Wisdom belongs to Jesus too,
Tho' he was charg'd with madness here.
[VERSE 5]
All riches are his native right,
Yet he sustain'd amazing loss:
To him ascribe eternal might,
Who left his weakness on the cross.
[VERSE 6]
Honour immortal must be paid,
Instead of scandal and of scorn:
While glory shines around his head,
And a bright crown without a thorn.
[VERSE 7]
Blessings for ever on the Lamb,
Who bore the curse for wretched men:
Let angels sound his sacred Name,
And every creature say, Amen.

--- TITLE: Adoption, 1 John 3. 1 &c. Gal. 4. 6
[VERSE 1]
Adoption, 1 John 3. 1 &c. Gal. 4. 6.
[VERSE 2]
Behold what wondrous grace
The Father hath bestow'd
On sinners of a mortal race,
To call them sons of God!
[VERSE 3]
'Tis no surprising thing
That we should be unknown;
The Jewish world knew not their King,
God's everlasting Son.
[VERSE 4]
Nor doth it yet appear
How great we must be made;
But when we see our Saviour here,
We shall be like our head.
[VERSE 5]
A hope so much divine
May trials well endure,
May purge our souls from sense and sin,
As Christ the Lord is pure.
[VERSE 6]
If in my Father's love
I share a filial part,
Send down thy Spirit like a dove
To rest upon my heart.
[VERSE 7]
We would no longer lie
Like slaves beneath the throne;
My faith shall Abba, Father, cry,
And thou the kindred own.

--- TITLE: The kingdoms of the world become the kingdoms
[VERSE 1]
The kingdoms of the world become the kingdoms
of our Lord; or, The day of judgment, Rev. 11. 15-18.
[VERSE 2]
Let the seventh angel sound on high,
Let shouts be heard thro' all the sky;
Kings of the earth, with glad accord
Give up your kingdoms to the Lord.
[VERSE 3]
Almighty God, thy power assume,
Who wast, and art, and art to come:
Jesus, the Lamb, who once was slain,
For ever live, for ever reign.
[VERSE 4]
The angry nations fret and roar,
That they can slay the saints no more;
On wings of vengeance flies our God
To pay the long arrears of blood.
[VERSE 5]
Now must the rising dead appear,
Now the decisive sentence hear;
Now the dear martyrs of the Lord
Receive an infinite reward.

--- TITLE: Christ the King at his table, Cant. (Transcriber's Note
[VERSE 1]
Christ the King at his table, Cant. (Transcriber's Note:
Song of Solomon) 1. 2-5 12 13 17.
[VERSE 2]
Let him embrace my soul, and prove
Mine interest in his heavenly love:
The voice that tells me, "Thou art mine,"
Exceeds the blessings of the vine.
[VERSE 3]
On thee th' anointing Spirit came,
And spreads the savour of thy name;
That oil of gladness and of grace
Draws virgin souls to meet thy face.
[VERSE 4]
Jesus, allure me by thy charms,
My soul shall fly into thine arms,
Our wandering feet thy favours bring
To the fair chambers of the King.
[VERSE 5]
Wonder and pleasure tune our voice
To speak thy praises and our joys:
Our memory keeps this love of thine
Beyond the taste of richest wine.
[VERSE 6]
Tho' in ourselves deform'd we are,
And black as Kedar tent appear,
Yet when we put thy beauties on,
Fair as the courts of Solomon.
[VERSE 7]
While at his table sits the King,
He loves to see us smile and sing;
Our graces are our best perfume,
And breathe like spikenard round the room.
[VERSE 8]
As myrrh new bleeding from the tree,
Such is a dying Christ to me;
And while he makes my soul his guest,
My bosom, Lord, shall be thy rest.
[VERSE 9]
No beams of cedar or of fir
Can with thy courts on earth compare;
And here we wait until thy love
Raise us to nobler seats above.

--- TITLE: Seeking the pastures of Christ the Shepherd, Cant. 1.7
[VERSE 1]
Seeking the pastures of Christ the Shepherd, Cant. 1.7.
[VERSE 2]
Thou whom my soul admires above
All earthly joy, and earthly love,
Tell me, dear shepherd, let me know,
Where doth thy sweetest pasture grow?
[VERSE 3]
Where is the shadow of that rock
That from the son defends thy flock?
Fain would I feed among thy sheep,
Among them rest, among them sleep.
[VERSE 4]
Why should thy bride appear like one
That turns aside to paths unknown?
My constant feet would never rove,
Would never seek another love.
[VERSE 5]
The footsteps of thy flock I see;
Thy sweetest pastures here they be;
A wondrous feast thy love prepares,
Bought with thy wounds, and groans, and tears.
[VERSE 6]
His dearest flesh he makes my food,
And bids me drink his richest blood;
Here to these hills my soul will come,
To my beloved lead me home.

--- TITLE: The banquet of love, Cant. 2. 1 2 3 4 6 7
[VERSE 1]
The banquet of love, Cant. 2. 1 2 3 4 6 7.
[VERSE 2]
Behold the Rose of Sharon here,
The Lily which the vallies bear;
Behold the Tree of Life, that gives
Refreshing fruit and healing leaves.
[VERSE 3]
Amongst the thorns so lilies shine,
Amongst wild gourds the noble vine;
So in mine eyes my Saviour proves
Amidst a thousand meaner loves.
[VERSE 4]
Beneath his cooling shade I sat
To shield me from the burning heat;
Of heavenly fruit he spreads a feast
To feed my eyes and please my taste.
[VERSE 5]
Kindly he brought me to the place
Where stands the banquet of his grace,
He saw me faint, and o'er my head
The banner of his love he spread.
[VERSE 6]
With living bread and generous wine
He cheers this sinking heart of mine;
And opening his own heart to me,
He shews his thoughts how kind they be.
[VERSE 7]
O never let my Lord depart,
Lie down and rest upon my heart;
I charge my sins not once to move
Nor stir, nor wake, nor grieve my Love.

--- TITLE: Christ appearing to his church, and seeking her
[VERSE 1]
Christ appearing to his church, and seeking her
company, Cant. 2. 1-13.
[VERSE 2]
The voice of my beloved sounds
Over the rocks and rising grounds,
O'er hills of guilt, and seas of grief,
He leaps, he flies to my relief.
[VERSE 3]
Now thro' the veil of flesh I see
With eyes of love he looks at me;
Now in the gospel's clearest glass
He shews the beauties of his face.
[VERSE 4]
Gently he draws my heart along
Both with his beauties and his tongue;
"Rise," saith my Lord, "make haste away;
"No mortal joys are worth thy stay.
[VERSE 5]
"The Jewish wintery state is gone,
"The mists are fled, the spring comes on,
"The sacred turtle-dove we hear
"Proclaim the new, the joyful year.
[VERSE 6]
"Th' immortal vine of heavenly root
"Blossoms and buds, and gives her fruit:"
Lo, we are come to taste the wine;
Our souls rejoice and bless the vine.
[VERSE 7]
And when we hear our Jesus say,
"Rise up, my love, make haste away!"
Our hearts would fain out-fly the wind,
And leave all earthly loves behind.

--- TITLE: Christ inviting, and the church answering the
[VERSE 1]
Christ inviting, and the church answering the
invitation, Cant. 2. 14 16 17.
[VERSE 2]
Hark, the Redeemer from on high
Sweetly invites his favourites nigh;
From caves of darkness and of doubt,
He gently speaks, and calls us out:
[VERSE 3]
"My dove, who hidest in the rock,
"Thine heart almost with sorrow broke,
"Lift up thy face, forget thy fear,
"And let thy voice delight mine ear.
[VERSE 4]
"Thy voice to me sounds ever sweet;
"My graces in thy countenance meet;
"Tho' the vain world thy face despise,
"'Tis bright and comely in mine eyes."
[VERSE 5]
Dear Lord, our thankful heart receives
The hope thine invitation gives:
'To thee our joyful lips shall raise
The voice of prayer, and of praise.
[VERSE 6]
I am my Love's, and he is mine;
Our hearts, our hopes, our passions join:
Nor let a motion, nor a word,
Nor thought arise to grieve my Lord.
[VERSE 7]
My soul to pastures fair he leads,
Amongst the lilies where he feeds;
Amongst the saints (whose robes are white,
Wash'd in his blood) is his delight.
[VERSE 8]
Till the day break, and shadows flee,
Till the sweet dawning light I see,
Thine eyes to me-ward often turn,
Nor let my soul in darkness mourn.
[VERSE 9]
Be like a hart on mountains green,
Leap o'er the hills of fear and sin;
Nor guilt, nor unbelief divide
My Love, my Saviour from my side.

--- TITLE: Christ found in the street, and brought to the church
[VERSE 1]
Christ found in the street, and brought to the church,
Cant.	3. 1-5.
[VERSE 2]
Often I seek my Lord by night,
Jesus, my love, my soul's delight;
With warm desire and restless thought
I seek him oft, but find him not.
[VERSE 3]
Then I arise, and search the street
Till I my Lord, my Saviour meet;
I ask the watchmen of the night,
"Where did you see my soul's delight?"
[VERSE 4]
Sometimes I find him in the way,
Directed by a heavenly ray;
I leap for joy to see his face,
And hold him fast in mine embrace.
[VERSE 5]
I bring him to my mother's home,
Nor does my Lord refuse to come,
To Sion's sacred chambers, where
My soul first drew the vital air.
[VERSE 6]
He gives me there his bleeding heart,
Pierc'd for my sake with deadly smart;
I give my soul to him, and there
Our loves their mutual tokens share.
[VERSE 7]
I charge you all, ye earthly toys,
Approach not to disturb my joys;
Nor sin, nor hell come near my heart,
Nor cause my Saviour to depart.

--- TITLE: The coronation of Christ, and espousals of the church
[VERSE 1]
The coronation of Christ, and espousals of the church,
Cant. 3. 2.
[VERSE 2]
Daughters of Sion, come, behold
The crown of honour and of gold,
Which the glad church with joys unknown
Plac'd on the head of Solomon.
[VERSE 3]
Jesus, thou everlasting King,
Accept the tribute which we bring,
Accept the well-deserv'd renown,
And wear our praises as thy crown.
[VERSE 4]
Let every act of worship be
Like our espousals, Lord, to thee;
Like the dear hour, when from above
We first receiv'd thy pledge of love.
[VERSE 5]
The gladness of that happy day,
Our hearts would wish it long to stay,
Nor let our faith forsake its hold,
Nor comfort sink, nor love grow cold.
[VERSE 6]
Each following minute as it flies,
Increase thy praise, improve our joys,
Till we are rais'd to sing thy Name
At the great supper of the Lamb.
[VERSE 7]
O that the months would roll away,
And bring that coronation-day!
The King of Grace shall fill the throne
With all his Father's glories on.

--- TITLE: The church's beauty in the eyes of Christ
[VERSE 1]
The church's beauty in the eyes of Christ,
Cant. 4. 1 10 11 7 9 8.
[VERSE 2]
Kind is the speech of Christ our Lord,
Affection sounds in every word,
"Lo, thou art fair, my love, he cries,
"Not the young doves have sweeter eyes.
[VERSE 3]
"Sweet are thy lips, thy pleasing voice
"Salutes mine ear with secret joys,
"No spice so much delights the smell,
"Nor milk nor honey taste so well.
[VERSE 4]
"Thou art all fair, my bride, to me,
"I will behold no spot in thee."
What mighty wonders love performs,
And puts a comeliness on worms!
[VERSE 5]
Defil'd and loathsome as we are,
He makes us white, and calls us fair;
Adorns us with that heavenly dress,
His graces and his righteousness.
[VERSE 6]
"My sister, and my spouse," he cries,
"Bound to my heart by various ties,
"Thy powerful love my heart detains
"In strong delight and pleasing chains."
[VERSE 7]
He calls me from the leopard's den,
From this wild world of beasts and men,
To Sion where his glories are;
Not Lebanon is half so fair.
[VERSE 8]
Nor dens of prey, nor flowery plains
Nor earthly joys, nor earthly pains
Shall hold my feet, or force my stay,
When Christ invites my soul away.

--- TITLE: The church the garden of Christ, Cant. 4. 12 14 15
[VERSE 1]
The church the garden of Christ, Cant. 4. 12 14 15,
and 5. 1.
[VERSE 2]
We are a garden wall'd around,
Chosen and made peculiar ground;
A little spot inclos'd by grace,
Out of the world's wide wilderness.
[VERSE 3]
Like trees of myrrh and spice we stand,
Planted by God the Father's hand;
And all his springs in Sion flow
To make the young plantation grow.
[VERSE 4]
Awake, O heavenly wind, and come,
Blow on this garden of perfume;
Spirit divine, descend and breathe
A gracious gale on plants beneath.
[VERSE 5]
Make our best spices flow abroad
To entertain our Saviour God:
And faith, and love, and joy appear,
And every grace be active here.
[VERSE 6]
Let my beloved come, and taste
His pleasant fruits at his own feast:
"I come, my spouse, I come," he cries,
With love and pleasure in his eyes.
[VERSE 7]
Our Lord into his garden comes,
Well pleas'd to smell our poor perfumes;
And calls us to a feast divine,
Sweeter than honey, milk, or wine.
[VERSE 8]
"Eat of the tree of life, my friends,
"The blessings that my Father sends;
"Your taste shall all my dainties prove,
"And drink abundance of my love."
[VERSE 9]
Jesus, we will frequent thy board,
And sing the bounties of our Lord:
But the rich food on which we live
Demands more praise than tongues can give.

--- TITLE: The description of Christ the beloved
[VERSE 1]
The description of Christ the beloved,
Cant. 5. 9-12 14 15 16.
[VERSE 2]
The wondering world enquires to know
Why I should love my Jesus so:
"What are his charms," say they, "above
"The objects of a mortal love!"
[VERSE 3]
Yes, my beloved, to my sight,
Shews a sweet mixture red and white:
All human beauties, all divine,
In my beloved meet and shine,
[VERSE 4]
White is his soul, from blemish free;
Red with the blood he shed for me;
The fairest of ten thousand fairs:
A sun amongst ten thousand stars.
[VERSE 5]
His head the finest gold excels,
There wisdom in perfection dwells;
And glory like a crown adorns
Those temples once beset with thorns.
[VERSE 6]
Compassions in his heart are found,
Hard by the signals of his wound;
His sacred side no more shall bear
The cruel scourge, the piercing spear.
[VERSE 7]
His hands are fairer to behold
Than diamonds set in rings of gold;
Those heavenly hands that on the tree
Were nail'd, and torn, and bled for me.
[VERSE 8]
Tho' once he bow'd his feeble knees,
Loaded with sins and agonies,
Now on the throne of his command
His legs like marble pillars stand.
[VERSE 9]
His eyes are majesty and love,
The eagle temper'd with the dove:
No more shall trickling sorrows roll
Thro' those dear windows of his soul.
[VERSE 10]
His mouth, that pour'd out long complaints,
Now smiles, and cheers his fainting saints;
His countenance more graceful is
Than Lebanon with all its trees.
[VERSE 11]
All over glorious is my Lord,
Must be belov'd, and yet ador'd:
His worth if all the nations knew,
Sure the whole earth would love him too.

--- TITLE: Christ dwells in heaven, but visits on earth
[VERSE 1]
Christ dwells in heaven, but visits on earth,
Cant. 6. 1 2 3 12.
[VERSE 2]
When strangers stand and hear me tell
What beauties in my Saviour dwell;
Where he is gone, they fain would know,
That they may seek and love him too.
[VERSE 3]
My best beloved keeps his throne
On hills of light in worlds unknown
But he descends, and shews his face
In the young gardens of his grace.
[VERSE 4]
In vineyards planted by his hand,
Where fruitful trees in order stand;
He feeds among the spicy beds,
Where lilies shew their spotless heads.
[VERSE 5]
He has engross'd my warmest love,
No earthly charms my soul can move:
I have a mansion in his heart,
Nor death nor hell shall make us part.
[VERSE 6]
He takes my soul ere I'm aware,
And shews me where his glories are;
No chariot of Amminadib
The heavenly rapture can describe.
[VERSE 7]
O may my spirit daily rise
On wings of faith above the skies,
Till death shall make my last remove
To dwell for ever with my love.

--- TITLE: The love of Christ to the church, in his language to
[VERSE 1]
The love of Christ to the church, in his language to
her, and provisions for her, Cant. 7. 5 6 9 12 13.
[VERSE 2]
Now in the galleries of his grace
Appears the King, and thus he says,
"How fair my saints are in my sight;
"My love how pleasant for delight!"
[VERSE 3]
Kind is thy language, sovereign Lord,
